Method is governed by **`docs/audit/IH35-FULL-SYSTEM-AUDIT-SPEC.md` (v3.1 · 2026-08-03)** — DoD A–E + VERIFY 1–8, live-exercised. Rule 13 scope-matched tie-out. Companion: `AUDIT-LAW-PERMANENT.md`. **"Complete" is NOT five layers** and is NOT a screenshot / route string / count — it is all applicable 13 gates PROD-VERIFIED per entity (V2 picker+creator, V3 wiring, V4 deep linkage F+R mandatory). Order C→B→A→D→E for record DoD; VERIFY V1–V8 per §B8/§B12 of the full-system spec.

# AUDIT COVERAGE — LIVE (single source of truth)

**This file is the work list.** Before ANY block, read it. Your work list = the rows where
`Verdict = FAIL` **and** `Status = OPEN` **and** the lane is yours.

**Before starting a class/finding, RESERVE it in `RESERVATION-LEDGER.md`** (multi-writer, carved out of Rule 28). Draw verify-step numbers from your own CLAIM-BLOCK. On a Scoreboard conflict: union the Findings rows and regenerate — never hand-merge. Protocol: `docs/audit/RESERVATION-LEDGER-PROTOCOL-2026-08-05.md`.

Established 2026-08-02 · **APPEND-ONLY** · one file, many writers.

---

## Column ownership — never edit another role's column

| Role | Owns | Write mode |
|---|---|---|
| **CASCADE** | `Module` · `Layer` · `Entity` · `Verdict` · `Evidence` · `Date` · `Auditor` | **APPENDS new rows only** |
| **CODER / CURSOR** | `Status` · `Block/PR` | only on rows **in their lane**; only `FIXED (PR #nnnn)` |
| **GUARD** | `Status` → `VERIFIED` / `REOPENED` | **only GUARD writes `VERIFIED`** |

**Rules of the file**
1. **Sync first.** `git pull --ff-only origin main` before you write — the row you edit must be current.
2. **Commit + push immediately** after writing. Small atomic docs-only commit. Non-financial → self-merge on green CI.
3. **NEVER delete a row.** Supersede = append a new dated row and mark the old one `SUPERSEDED`.
4. **A row with no `Evidence` is not a finding — it does not count.** Evidence means live proof produced
   this session: a prod read with its positive control, a file:line, an endpoint response, a health SHA.
5. **`Owner-gate?`** = `YES` when the row cannot be closed by a coder because it needs an owner DECISION
   (canonical pick, account designation, legal call, opening balance). Coders do not self-answer these.
6. **Report TRANSP and USMCA separately.** Every verdict is TRANSP-only unless the `Entity` cell says otherwise.
7. **`Module` = canonical sidebar id** from `SIDEBAR_ITEM_IDS` (30). Sub-module rows tag the parent:
   `accounting · bills`, `settlements · damage_recovery`. No free-text module names.
8. **Scoreboard is generated** — never hand-edit. After appending rows: `node scripts/audit-coverage-scoreboard.mjs --write`.
   CI fails if the Scoreboard drifts from the Findings rows.


**Layers:** `A` chrome/parity · `B` module close (confirmed FAIL / UNVERIFIED) · `C` deep linkage + GL ·
`D` picker law · `E` UI/design. No module is a certified PASS until all five pass independently, with live evidence.

**Read discipline (doc 06 + Rule 10):** a `0`/empty is not proof. Counts are read on Neon prod branch
`br-fancy-credit-akjnd07a`, and a `0` is a verdict ONLY when the **completeness discriminator** holds on the
SAME table: `visible == n_live_tup` (`pg_stat_all_tables`), `n_tup_del = 0` (genuinely live, not
inserted-then-deleted), `current_user` asserted **in the same statement**, and the positive control on that
same table.

**Do NOT rely on `app.bypass_rls='lucia'` plus a positive control on a different table.** That was the
method used for earlier rows here and it is unsound: the bypass is **INERT on AND-gated policies** and returns
`0` silently, so a control proving the bypass worked on `mdata.vendors` proves nothing about the table being
read. Rows below written before 2026-08-03 carry that weaker method — re-verify with the discriminator before
citing one as absence.

**SCOPE-MATCHED TIE-OUT LAW (2026-08-03 — kills CLS-FUEL-DOUBLE-POST class of false money findings):**
A dollar gap between two ledgers is **not** a double-post (and not publishable as one) unless both sides of the
comparison share the **same scope**: same source population, same entity, same date window, same account /
category filter, and the same completeness discriminator. **Forbidden:** comparing a source-specific subledger
(e.g. Relay-card-only `fuel.fuel_transactions`) against an **all-sources** GL account (Fuel Expense receiving
bank categorizations, Love's, Comdata, tires/tolls miscoded, etc.) and calling the difference a double-post.
That is a **scope mismatch**. Before publishing any `CLS-*-DOUBLE-POST` / double-count money finding: (1) prove
amount+date (or stronger) matches > 0 with the discriminator applied, **or** (2) retract and reclassify
(scope mismatch vs miscategorization vs true dual write). A scope mismatch may still hide a real defect
(e.g. tires/tolls mapped into Fuel) — that defect is `CLS-CATEGORY-MAP-COHERENCE`, not double-post.

| 224 | system · resize_discoverability | E | ALL | FAIL (design) | **Cross-module finding:** Column resize handles across ALL ParityTable instances use a 6px transparent strip (`w-1.5 cursor-col-resize`). The handle is **invisible at rest** — only becomes visible on hover (`hover:bg-gray-300`). A user has no visual affordance that columns are resizable unless they accidentally mouse over the exact 6px border. QBO shows a visible column border with resize cursor; this TMS hides it entirely. This affects ALL 14 modules using ParityTable (Invoices, Bills, Fleet, Maintenance, Vendors, Customers, Compliance, Inventory, Docs, ELD, Reports, Tasks, Safety, Banking). | FIXED (PR #4027) | — | NO | 2026-08-02 | CASCADE |

| 595 | accounting · bill_validator | C | ALL | FAIL — no vendor bill with a category line could EVER save (ACCT-F93, P1) | `VendorBillForm` validated `line.account_id` but `buildVendorBillLinePayloads` DECLARES `account_id` on the type and ASSIGNS IT NOWHERE. Predicate unconditionally TRUE for every Section-A line → "Each Category line needs an expense category" error fired regardless of selection. EVERY vendor bill with category cost lines was blocked. Fix: validate `expense_category_uuid` (the field the grid writes). NOT the combobox — the combobox commits correctly via `expense_category_uuid`. **Re-baseline 2026-08-03:** PR #4062 merged 2026-08-02 23:35Z, deployed SHA `0a50b83`. 2 new bills created post-fix and posted balanced JEs (rows 619/633). | FIXED (PR #4062) | #4062 | NO | 2026-08-03 | GUARD |

Fix: repoint FK + route to canonical `mdata.vendors` (migration 202611170000). Also fixed: `reports.routes.ts:109` LEFT JOINed mirror on canonical FK → report fell through to raw UUID. **Re-baseline 2026-08-03:** PR #4048 merged 2026-08-02 23:34Z, deployed SHA `0a50b83`. 0 new WOs created post-fix (row 621 still live-exercise blocker). | FIXED (PR #4048) | #4048 | NO | 2026-08-03 | GUARD |

| 678 | safety · SAF-F16 driver fines reverse surface must read BOTH fines tables | C | ALL | FAIL | The driver profile carried a dozen ops views and none for fines, so money taken off a driver's settlement had no reverse surface on the driver it was taken from. The trap is **under-reporting, not absence**: there are TWO fines tables and they are not interchangeable — `safety.civil_fines` keyed `subject_driver_id`, `safety.internal_fines` keyed **`driver_id`** (the wave card said `subject_driver_id` for both; the real column is `driver_id`, verified in `safety-v5.routes.ts`). A panel reading only one renders, looks wired, and shows a partial list — and a partial fines list reads as complete. Backend already supported both sides server-side, so this was purely an unrendered component. **FIX:** `DriverFinesReverseSection` reads both sources, each scoped **server-side** (the internal-fines route caps at LIMIT 500, so a client-side filter would silently drop fines past the cap), reports per-source failure so one dead call cannot masquerade as "no fines", and is embedded on `DriverProfilePage`. Civil fines drill through `EntityLink kind="safety_fine"` (resolves); internal fines deliberately get **no** per-id link because `/safety/internal-fines` does not honour a per-id param and fabricating a kind would break EntityLink's contract for every caller. Guard `scripts/verify-driver-fines-reverse-surface.mjs` (step 2361), 9/9 mutations detected. | FIXED (PR #4311) | #4311 | NO | 2026-08-04 | CLAUDE-1 |
| 679 | drivers · CLS-SILENT-CAP inst.1 — roster CSV export 400'd silently, it did not truncate | C | ALL | FAIL | The card reported "roster > 500 drivers silently truncated in CSV export". **The real defect is worse.** The handler requested `limit: 500` in ONE call while the backend clamps limit to `.max(200)` (`mdata/drivers.routes.ts` `listQuerySchema`), so the request never truncated — it **failed zod validation and returned 400**. The handler had no `catch`, so the rejection was swallowed and only `exporting` reset: clicking "Export profiles (CSV)" produced **no file and no error**, while the comment directly above claimed it exported "the FULL driver roster". A silent FAILURE reading as a no-op is worse than the silent cap described. **FIX:** pages at the backend's real maximum and continues until every offset up to the server's own `total` is covered, refuses to download a file known to be short, and surfaces failures via toast. Paging walks **OFFSETS** rather than comparing collected row counts on purpose: `listDrivers` filters system rows client-side while `total` is the server's unfiltered count, so a length comparison would under-count on any roster containing a system driver and would have refused **every** export — trading a silent truncation for a silent refusal. Guard `scripts/verify-driver-roster-export-complete.mjs` (step 2363) reads the cap **from the backend schema at runtime** rather than hardcoding 200, and scans every `listDrivers` caller for an over-limit literal; scoped to `listDrivers` only because other entities' caps are unverified and asserting a shared number would be a guess. | FIXED (PR #4307) | #4307 | NO | 2026-08-04 | CLAUDE-1 |

Root cause of the collision itself: `accounting.invoices` carries `UNIQUE (operating_company_id, display_id)` — **by design, each entity's sequence restarts at 00001 independently**; this is a normal per-tenant numbering scheme, not a broken constraint. The hazard is exactly as the work order states: `display_id` is **not a safe standalone key** anywhere in the app — every call site MUST co-key it with `operating_company_id` or it can resolve the wrong entity's row. **Code sweep this pass (full grep of `apps/backend/src` for `display_id` in a `WHERE`/lookup position, plus the frontend for a display_id-driven `.find()`/void action):** the canonical void engine (`apps/backend/src/accounting/void.service.ts`) never resolves by `display_id` at all — it takes a UUID `target_id` plus an explicit `operatingCompanyId` param and every query is `WHERE operating_company_id = $1 AND id = $2`; the two other exact-match `display_id` lookups found (`apps/backend/src/factoring/faro-csv-import.ts:298-307` and `:381-402`) are both correctly co-scoped (`WHERE operating_company_id = $1::uuid AND display_id = $2::text`); every `display_id ILIKE` search-box hit (`invoices.routes.ts:209`, `payments.routes.ts:167`, `factoring-advances.routes.ts:196`, `cash-advances.routes.ts:173`) sits inside a `withCompanyScope(...)`-wrapped list endpoint, i.e. layered on top of an opco-scoped base query, not a replacement for one. **I could not find or reproduce the "a display_id void would have voided a real paid $3,800 TRANSP invoice" scenario in current code** — no unscoped call site exists on the paths I checked. **INSTANCE LIST (per-entity numbered docs, sweep so far):** `accounting.invoices` — collision CONFIRMED (4 pairs above), all found lookups SAFE (see above). `accounting.bills`, `driver_finance.driver_settlements`, `accounting.factoring_advances` — checked for the same duplicate-`display_id`-across-entity pattern: **0 duplicates** in all three live tables today (no current collision to exploit even if an unscoped lookup existed). Class card `CLS-DISPLAYID-UNSCOPED` opened in `wave-queue.json` as a **ratchet/regression guard**, not a live-exploit card: the risk is real (schema allows the collision, so any FUTURE or as-yet-unfound code path that trusts `display_id` alone is one bad merge away from cross-entity action) even though this pass found none live. Marked `UNVERIFIED` rather than `FAIL` because the work order's specific proof (the $3,800 void repro) was not independently located — if the author has the file/line for that repro, append it and flip this row to `FAIL`. **★ GUARD ADDENDUM 2026-08-06 (CC-2, row 689) — the missing call site is FOUND, and this row's sweep had a directional blind spot.** The collision count is independently re-confirmed at **4** (matches this row exactly). The unscoped site is `apps/backend/src/accounting/customer-payments.routes.ts` — the LATERAL `JOIN accounting.invoices i ON i.id = pa.invoice_id` with no entity predicate, which **EMITS** `invoice_display_id` onto a payment application; fixed in **#4536** (`19ed1a4d3`), mutation-proven under guard step **2648**. **Why this row's sweep missed it:** the sweep searched `display_id` in a `WHERE`/lookup position — i.e. display_id as an **INPUT** key. This site never uses display_id as a key; it joins by `id` and returns display_id as an **OUTPUT**. Those are two different hazards and only the input side was swept. This row's verdict on the input side stands (all `display_id`-keyed lookups found were correctly co-scoped). The **$3,800 VOID repro specifically remains NOT located** — `void.service.ts` still resolves by UUID + explicit `operatingCompanyId`, so this row stays `OPEN`/`UNVERIFIED` on the void claim rather than flipping to `FAIL`; what is now proven is the mislabel hazard (another entity's invoice NUMBER stamped on a payment application), not a cross-entity void. Output-side sweep of the remaining display_id-emitting joins is filed as an OPEN board row for CC-3. | OPEN | — | NO | 2026-08-05 | CASCADE |
